| **D03-FR-06** | Supplier bank-change verification (mandatory fraud control, links M06/M15) | **WIRED** | Verified out-of-band, dated, recorded as an event in `services/purchase` + `packages/bank-controls`; not a silent drop in code, only in the RTM |

## Design artifacts (Stage 3–4)

Store-Core requirements are now covered at **design level** by the artifacts below; the
individual rows keep `Design → docs/requirements/M##.md` as their requirement anchor and
stay **In design** until code + tests exist (Definition of Done). These are the design
references those rows resolve to:

| Artifact | Location | Covers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Screen specs (all 14 §27 surfaces) | `docs/design/screens/` + `docs/design/design-system.md` | UX/QG-02 for every role surface |
| Architecture overview | `docs/architecture/README.md` | §19 planes, bounded contexts → API-01…13 |
| Data model (§29) | `docs/architecture/data-model.md` | entity/rule model, one-commerce-truth |
| API & event catalogue (§30) | `docs/api/catalogue.md` | API-01…13, conventions, §30.2 events |
| Offline-sync design (§31) | `docs/architecture/offline-sync.md` | P-01 detail, sync protocol, conflicts |
| Migration design (§34) | `docs/architecture/migration-design.md` | MG-01…12 pipeline, QG-07 control totals |
| Count verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, §28) | `packages/migration/src/count-verification.ts` | Proving the extracted stock against the shelves — the only thing that can. **Value-stratified**, not random: the high-value lines are a CENSUS, the tail is sampled, because a random sample that happens to miss the ghee has verified almost nothing while looking thorough. Refuses a sample chosen by whoever ran the extraction (§28); the expected quantity never reaches the count sheet (`expectedQtyShownToCounter: false`); the draw is seeded so an auditor can ask why THIS line. The extrapolation is labelled an **estimate** and returns `undefined` rather than a fabricated figure when the sample cannot support one. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-count-verification.test.ts` |
| Verification report (§34, OB-06, MG-06, QG-07, P-08) | `packages/migration/src/verification-report.ts` | The one page the owner and the CA sign, gathering all six checks into the language of the runbook. The most dangerous file in the migration, because **a report is what people believe** — nobody re-derives a figure once a signed page exists — so the refusals are about the document rather than the arithmetic. **It cannot be produced from some of the domains**: four of twelve renders perfectly, every heading filled, and is a complete-looking account of a third of the business, so nothing renders until every domain has a finding. **An unproved domain is a row in the same table in the same type**, never a footnote (P-08). **A proved domain that states no limit is refused** — every check has a real limit, and a blank is read by whoever signs as "no limit". **Each exception is accepted one at a time, by name, by the owner, with a reason**, and a reason that fills the box without saying anything is refused as `packages/migration/src/cutover.ts` refuses a non-explanation. Whoever ran the extraction can neither prepare nor sign it (QG-07). Signatures accumulate and are never replaced — a signature belongs to the figures that were on the page when it was given. Renders Indian money grouping, every witness rather than the first, and counts where the domain is not a rupee total. Worked example in `docs/evidence/example-verification-report.md`; tested in `tests/unit/migration-verification-report.test.ts` |
| **OB-06 verification gate** (§34, OB-06, MG-06, QG-07) | `tests/integration/every-figure-has-a-witness.test.ts` | All six outside-evidence checks run as **one pass over one shop**, against real PostgreSQL 16.13. Adds the two things unit tests structurally cannot: it **walks the `VERIFIES` table for all twelve domains and proves every named source has a module behind it** — catching a domain with a named witness and no code, which is what a migration under time pressure produces; and it **proves the witnesses agree with each other**, tying four figures across checks (bank gross = filed taxable + tax; signed Stock = the counted shelves; signed Creditors = the suppliers' own confirmations; signed GST payable = the filed return), so a wrong number must be wrong consistently in two independent records to survive. The gate is proved to **fail**: withhold the physical count and five domains are refused by name as `verified_by_the_same_system`. The structural check is enforced by `Record<ExternalSource, string>`, so adding a kind of evidence with no check built is a **compile error**, not merely a red test. Verified figures are banked as append-only events carrying the witness that proved them. 16 assertions |
| Loyalty verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, M12, §28) | `packages/migration/src/loyalty-verification.ts` | The last of the six and the odd one out: **no third party keeps a record of a customer's points**, so the only witness is the customer. Two facts about customers decide the design. **The feedback is asymmetric, so complaints are a useless sample** — a customer whose points fell complains, one whose points rose never does, so a sample drawn from complaints is 100% shortfalls by construction and would confirm a systematically over-crediting migration with real evidence from real customers. **Refused by name.** And **the loud direction is not the dangerous one**: understated points are visible, immediate and self-correcting because they generate complaints; overstated points are silent, permanent, redeemed in goods at our cost, and never discovered — so the silent side sorts first, is valued at the tenant's own cost per redeemed point, and is never netted against the loud side. The central control mirrors the stock count: **the balance is never shown to the customer** (`balanceShownToTheCustomer: false`), because asked *"is it 450?"* almost anybody says yes — what is accepted is the customer's own figure or the activity they remember. Sampling is seeded and three-strata (largest balances, tier boundaries, random tail), with the census winning over the boundary rule so coverage is never double-counted. Tier changes are named — a tier is on every receipt. `provesTheBalanceWasEarned` is typed as the literal `false`. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-loyalty-verification.test.ts` |
| Books verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, M16) | `packages/migration/src/books-verification.ts` | The signed closing balance sheet **is** the opening position, so this ties the other five checks together — stock, debtors, creditors, cash and tax all appear on it, each already proved by its own evidence. Also, stated plainly, **the weakest of the six as independent evidence**: the CA prepared them from the same old system, so what they add is a professional signature and the discipline of double entry, not an independent source (`provesTheAccountsAreRight: false`). **A balancing figure is refused by name** — Suspense, Opening Difference, Diff A/c, plug — because posting the difference there makes the books balance and be wrong, and that account is never cleared. Checked **before** the balance test, so a book that closes only because of the plug is never reported as balancing. Also refused: draft (unsigned) accounts; accounts that do not themselves balance; a cutover that is not the day after the accounts' period end (out by a whole trading period while looking authoritative); and the absence of what **only the CA has** — depreciation, provisions, accruals, prepayments, drawings — whose absence is not a variance but exactly the amount that would end up in suspense. Contra accounts (drawings, accumulated depreciation) are known, so the wrong-side flag does not fire on every correct set of books. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-books-verification.test.ts` |
| Tax verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, M23) | `packages/migration/src/tax-verification.ts` | The only check that runs **backwards**: a filed return is true as a matter of law, so the question is not whether it agrees with the books but what the books must become. Four refusals, each load-bearing: **no acknowledgement reference** — a spreadsheet named after a return is a working paper, and the ARN is the only thing separating filed from prepared; **not a whole tax period** — a part period is short by design and reads as missing sales; **superseded by an amendment** — reconciling to the restated original gives a wrong answer with a flawless audit trail; **the return's own arithmetic failing** — reconciling to it would spread the error through every opening figure. **A slab rate is never inferred from a total**: an average across a mixed basket is not a rate anything could have been sold at, so a books line off the statutory slabs is refused, and no entry point taking a total and a rate exists (proven by scanning the module's real exports). GSTR-1 is checked against GSTR-3B as well as against the books, because the department reconciles those two automatically — a difference there is **inherited, not created**. A difference against a filed return is a **disclosure to the CA**, not a private data fix. `provesTaxWasCorrectlyCharged` is typed as the literal `false`. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-tax-verification.test.ts` |
| Extraction tool (§34, OB-06, MG-01/02/05) | `scripts/extract-check.mts` | **The door.** Every verification module in `packages/migration` was unreachable by anybody not writing TypeScript; this takes a file exactly as the incumbent produced it and runs the checks in the runbook's order. **Completeness runs first and refuses** — a paginated grid that exported one page parses perfectly, sums to its own total to the paisa, and holds a tenth of the shop, so nothing else looks at the file until that is satisfied. Two defects found by pointing it at a realistic file: it split on the separator directly, and **Indian money is written with commas while these exports are comma-separated**, so `"1,28,000.00"` became three cells and only the figures that matter were destroyed — while the row count still looked right; and it added one to `headerLine`, which the parser already numbers 1-based so a person can quote it. Run as a real subprocess in `tests/integration/the-extraction-tool-runs.test.ts`, because a tool tested only through its library is a tool whose arguments, exit codes and output nobody has checked. Procedure: `docs/runbooks/extraction-work-plan.md` |
| Banking verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, §29.1) | `packages/migration/src/banking-verification.ts` | Sales proved against the bank, which has no interest in agreeing with our old ERP. Neither `packages/reconciliation` (needs a shared provider reference) nor `packages/settlement` (needs the provider's own file) can answer this for a historic period, so the route is **reconstructed**: cash lodged in lumps days later, card net of commission and the GST on it, UPI gross on its own cycle. The control the module stands on — **a commission rate is declared, never derived.** A rate computed as `(gross − banked) / gross` makes every shortfall commission by definition, so the check agrees perfectly at any figure; a test demonstrates a 60,000 hole reconciling to zero under a fitted rate, and the same input refused under `derived_from_the_difference`. **Cash is the dangerous direction** — the only tender a person carries, and the only difference with no counterparty who will ever chase it, so it carries its own figure plus the peak standing unlodged. **An unexplained credit is an exception, not a bonus**: migrated as revenue it overstates turnover and the tax due. The statement's span is read off its header, not inferred from its lines, and must run **past** the period end or the last day's batch is missing while the dates look matched. `provesSalesWereComplete` is typed as the literal `false`. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-banking-verification.test.ts` |
| Supplier reconciliation (§34, OB-06, MG-06) | `packages/migration/src/supplier-reconciliation.ts` | The external check that needs **nobody's goodwill** — the supplier keeps their own ledger and will confirm it, because they want paying. Two ledgers with no shared key that were never meant to agree at a point in time, so the job is telling a **timing difference** from a real one: within the window a payment in transit is the ordinary explanation, and past it, it is not. An `amount_differs` item is **never** timing, because both sides hold the document. Three rules that do not bend: **the dangerous direction is theirs** — an invoice only on their statement is a liability we would migrate as zero, so it is called out separately and sorted first; **nothing is netted**, because an unapplied payment and an unrecorded credit of the same size produce a clean zero that hides both; and **signs are mirrored deliberately** — every item carries its contribution to *theirs minus ours*, so the items sum to the headline difference and a reversed side cannot balance at twice the true figure. A supplier who never replied is named, not counted as agreeing. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-supplier-reconciliation.test.ts` |
| Export completeness (§34, OB-06, MG-01/05, P-08) | `packages/migration/src/completeness.ts` | The failure that RECONCILES PERFECTLY — a paginated grid exports one page, the file parses cleanly, its own grand total agrees to the paisa, and it holds 4,000 of 41,200 products. Four signals in order of trust: a row count taken off the screen; **`Page N of M`, which works when nobody wrote anything down**; the end marker, available only for a file that would have had one; and sequence density, deduped so repeats cannot inflate it. **Refused, not flagged.** `unverifiable` is a distinct verdict from `complete` — a file with no signals has failed to be caught, not passed. `compareDoubleKeyed` is Route D's control. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-completeness.test.ts` |
| Self-extraction round trip (§34, OB-06, MG-05) | `packages/migration/src/render-report.ts` | Renders a KNOWN dataset back into a printed report — banner, page breaks, repeated headers, department subtotals, Indian digit grouping — so the parser is measured against ground truth rather than against whether the answer looks plausible. `checkRoundTrip` names the product that went missing rather than counting rows short, and compares as a MULTISET so a lost second location cannot hide behind a still-present id. Proven end to end in `tests/integration/we-get-it-out-ourselves.test.ts`; tested in `tests/unit/migration-render-report.test.ts` |
| Legacy report parsing (§34, OB-06, §29.1) | `packages/migration/src/report-parser.ts` | What these systems ACTUALLY export — a printed page in a spreadsheet. Finds the header rather than assuming line 1; classifies subtotals FIRST so a group total is never counted as data (the misread that doubles a department); **Indian digit grouping** (4,12,000 is twelve lakh); **no float anywhere** — the decimal part is parsed as text, because `parseFloat(x)*100` turns 19.99 into 1998.9999999999998; `CR` read as negative so supplier balances keep their sign; a lone `-` read as nil. Nothing dropped silently, banner rows included. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-report-parser.test.ts` |
| Legacy self-extraction (§34, OB-06) | `packages/migration/src/extraction.ts` | **MG-01/02/06 without vendor cooperation** — four routes ranked by what they structurally lose, re-keying refused as a migration source because it cannot be re-run; and the control the approach stands on: a domain verified only against the system it came from is **refused by name**, so every total is proved against outside evidence (bank, filed returns, supplier statement, physical count). Procedure in `docs/runbooks/legacy-self-extraction.md`; tested in `tests/unit/migration-extraction.test.ts` |
| Migration engine (§34) | `packages/migration/` (`synthetic`, `discovery`, `mapping`, `cleaning`, `trial`, `reconcile`, `history`, `cutover`) | **MG-01…12 built and gated** — Stage 11 rehearsal against a synthetic legacy dataset with ten planted fault kinds, all found by identity; `docs/evidence/stage-11-the-old-shop-arrives-whole.md` |
| Threat & privacy model (§35) | `docs/security/threat-privacy-model.md` | trust boundaries, STRIDE, PRV, QG-06 |
| Security controls (SEC-02/03/12, PRV-03/05/08) | `tests/security/` (`access-control-sweep`, `separation-of-duties`, `data-protection`) | The **deny** path, swept rather than sampled: 240 access combinations against an oracle computed independently of the implementation; every self-authorisation point in the product in one list, each proven to allow with two people and refuse with one; card-data allowlist; erasure per category with the statute named. Found and fixed: `minimisePii` was a **blocklist wearing an allowlist's name** and passed `aadhaar_number` straight through; `AccessControl` held role permission lists by reference |
| Accessibility engine (NFR-07/13) | `packages/a11y/` (`contrast`, `signals`) | WCAG 2.2 AA contrast as ONE implementation for the product — fixed a shipped rounding defect that passed a failing 4.48:1 grey as 4.57:1; colour never the only signal (label + icon + announcement returned together, no `toneOf` helper exists); touch targets against both the WCAG 24px floor and the design system's 44px bar; the ≤3-interaction budget as an assertable number; focus order with every issue named at once |
| Performance budgets (§32/NFR-02/QG-05) | `tests/performance/` (`harness`, `pos-hot-path`, `sync-and-endurance`) | SHAPE not speed: scan proven O(1) at 100× catalogue, with a **tripwire that fires on a deliberate linear scan**; basket linear in its own lines; commit runs with the network globals removed (hard rule #1); outbox flat at 100× queue depth and 72h of trading; backlog drains in order exactly once. `certifiedHardwareGate()` records the six items that genuinely need EX-09 — `certifiesTheTarget` is typed as the literal `false` |
| Field-level data dictionary | `db/data-dictionary/` | six Store-Core domains, field level |

| Workforce — rosters, gated tasks, incentives & SOPs | M25-FR-01/02/03/04 / §28 / §29.1 / P-04 — a roster gap is NAMED with the hour ("Sunday 06:00 has NOBODY rostered as opener") rather than averaged into a coverage percentage nobody acts on, and a leaver still in the grid counts as no cover; a lapsed certification blocks the TASK and not the person, returning what they may still do, because blocking the person is how a shop routes around the system on a busy Saturday and a control people route around is not a control; blocking and non-blocking checklist items are separated so the one item that mattered is not ticked with the rest, and an unsigned checklist is a list not a record; incentives are exact integer arithmetic and a missed target pays NOTHING, since paying 96% for 96% redefines the target; labour cost is REPORTED, never enforced — a system that refuses a fourth cashier creates queues at Diwali, and no function exists that could refuse a roster on cost (asserted by test); acknowledging SOP v3 is not acknowledging v5 | `packages/workforce/src/workforce.ts` | `tests/unit/workforce.test.ts` (31) |
| B2B document chain — quote to tax invoice | M22-FR-02 / M01-FR-02 / M23 — each document is derived from the one before it and never from the one two steps back, because the chain drifting is how an invoice for 40 cases follows a van that carried 38; a quotation is non-committing and draws NO number when it refuses, since a gap in a tax series is a question from an assessing officer with no good answer; conversion is at the QUOTED price or refused outright rather than re-priced quietly at the invoice, and credit control is not optional; a proforma carries `taxClaimable: false` because input credit claimed against it is claimed against a document never filed; a challan carries what actually LEFT and over-delivery is refused; the tax invoice is built from the CHALLANS so a partial delivery bills partially — billing the ordered quantity is an overcharge with a tax invoice attached to it; the chain check names goods gone out with no claim on them | `packages/b2b/src/documents.ts` | `tests/unit/b2b-documents.test.ts` (21) |
| B2B portal, ageing & collections | M22-FR-04 / M23 / ADR-0003 / P-05 / hard rule #3 — the second outside login, scoped from the session with a cross-customer request refused as a security event; ageing runs from the DUE DATE and not the invoice date, because ageing from issue makes every account on terms look delinquent and then the report is ignored along with the genuinely overdue account; a payment is ALLOCATED to named invoices, never absorbed into a balance, with an overpayment held visibly unapplied; a disputed invoice is outstanding but never chaseable; stopping supply is RECOMMENDED and needs a person, because date arithmetic must not cut off a school on the morning of a function; the portal is reconciled to finance exactly and with its sign | `packages/b2b/src/collections.ts` | `tests/unit/b2b-collections.test.ts` (20) |
| Facilities — assets, downtime & energy | M26-FR-01/04 / §35 / D14 — criticality is a property of the ASSET not of the alert, so critical assets come back in their own list rather than merely sorted higher (a list where the cold room sits beside the shelf trolley is one where the cold room is missed); an expired AMC is reported against WHAT IT PROTECTS in money, because "AMC-14 expired" gets ignored and "₹80,000 of stock depends on it" gets renewed; a breakdown call is not preventive maintenance; downtime is measured from when it BROKE and unreported minutes are a number of their own, since an hour reported four hours late is a five-hour exposure; an energy figure states how much of it was ESTIMATED, because it will be quoted in a decision about buying a chiller. **WIRED on the cloud surface 7 Aug** — assets registered, services logged (breakdown does not reset the clock), downtime recorded and summarised from failure, energy readings kept with their source; health/downtime/energy read through the real API with real RBAC | `packages/facilities/src/assets.ts`, `services/platform/src/facilities-assets.ts`, `services/api/src/adapters.ts` | `tests/unit/facilities.test.ts` (39), `tests/integration/facilities-assets.test.ts` |
| Facilities — equipment & power monitoring | M26-FR-02 / D14 / M10 / P-08 — M10 assesses the BATCH, this assesses the EQUIPMENT: the store's habit is to probe a few batches and the room is what actually fails, so a breach names every batch in it and its value and holds them all, including the ones nobody probed; a sensor that has gone QUIET is a fault and not a pass, because the probe that fell out of the room three weeks ago has read as "no alerts" ever since — `no_data` and `stale` both hold; IoT is readiness rather than a dependency, so a hand-written log sheet and a sensor feed are assessed identically with the source recorded; power is assessed by WHAT IT PROTECTS, with unprotected minutes counted from the mains failure rather than the generator attempt because the stock does not care which equipment let it down. **WIRED on the cloud surface 7 Aug** — a range and current contents are set per equipment, readings arrive from sensor/manual-probe/log-sheet, power events recorded; `GET …/equipment/:id` returns the assessment (breach holds every batch, silence is `no_data`/`stale`) and `GET …/power` names the unprotected critical assets — all through the real API with real RBAC | `packages/facilities/src/monitoring.ts`, `services/platform/src/facilities-monitoring.ts`, `services/api/src/adapters.ts` | `tests/unit/facilities.test.ts` (39), `tests/integration/facilities-monitoring.test.ts` |
| Facilities — safety schedules, incidents & evidence | M26-FR-03/04 / P-03 / §28 / hard rule #6 — a tick without evidence is REFUSED rather than accepted with a note, since an accepted-with-a-note task shows green and green is what everybody reads; self-verification of a safety check is a signature against nothing; a missed compliance task ESCALATES by itself to a named person, because a task that quietly rolls into tomorrow's list forever is invisible precisely because nothing ever goes red — and cleaning is deliberately not compliance-linked so the fire check is not buried among forty mop-the-aisle alerts; an incident cannot be closed with no corrective action, by its own reporter, or (when reportable) with no statutory notification, because closing it internally is what makes everybody stop thinking about it; the evidence pack says plainly whether it would survive an inspection and names every gap. **WIRED on the cloud surface 7 Aug** — schedules/tasks/overdue and now incidents: `POST /v1/facilities/incidents/:id` raises one, `…/close` runs `closeIncident` (refuses no-action / no-evidence-for-serious / self-close / unreported-reportable, the closed record superseding the open one), and `GET /v1/facilities/evidence` runs `buildComplianceEvidence` — every gap named, `presentable` false while any remains | `packages/facilities/src/schedules.ts`, `services/platform/src/facilities.ts`, `services/api/src/adapters.ts` | `tests/unit/facilities.test.ts` (39), `tests/integration/facilities-schedules.test.ts`, `tests/integration/facilities-incidents.test.ts` |
| Concession & shop-in-shop | M27-FR-01/02/03/04 / §28 / P-04 / hard rules #2, #5 — ownership is a property of the STOCK and the valuation asks, because concession stock on our shelves swallowed into our valuation overstates the balance sheet, the insurance schedule and the tax position at once and always looks about right; what was excluded is named and valued, since a quiet exclusion is as dangerous as a quiet inclusion; store staff may see and (under contract) sell concession stock but never adjust or write it off; revenue share is exact BigInt arithmetic on sales net of refunds and `higher_of_both` is the HIGHER, never the sum; the money a concession sale takes was never our revenue — it is held on their behalf and discharged at settlement, and a banking difference is a valued exception; the deposit is stated but NEVER netted, projected from movements with an unapproved forfeit still a liability; lapsed insurance blocks new sales and every blocker is reported at once | `packages/concession/src/concession.ts` | `tests/unit/concession.test.ts` (30) |
| Scrap & recycling sales | M28-FR-02 / M23 / hard rule #6 — the one revenue stream in most shops with no paperwork and no controls, and because nobody knows what a month of cardboard is worth nobody can tell ₹4,000 from ₹12,000; the control is not suspicion, it is making the number EXIST; proceeds that have not reached the books are named as off-books cash whatever anyone intended; an unevidenced sale is FLAGGED and not refused, because refusing pushes the transaction back outside the system which is the problem we started with; rate drift is measured against the shop's OWN running average rather than a configured expectation that would be wrong within a quarter, needs enough history first, and asks about the RATE rather than the person; e-waste and used oil require a registered recycler | `packages/waste/src/scrap.ts` | `tests/unit/waste-sustainability.test.ts` (29) |
| Packaging, carry bags & reusable crates | M28-FR-03 / M12 / M05 / P-01 — a bag charge is a VISIBLE priced line or it does not exist, because an amount added to a total without a line is a consumer complaint the shop loses and showing it properly costs nothing; where the tenant has not enabled it there is NO line rather than a line worth nothing; the charge is pure arithmetic over the price pack the lane already holds, so it works with the internet down; a reusable crate is an asset IN CIRCULATION rather than a consumable, so 118 crates unaccounted for is a number instead of a feeling and the shop stops buying 400 again every year; a negative position is reported NEGATIVE rather than clamped, because the negative is the only evidence that a goods-in was never entered | `packages/waste/src/packaging.ts` | `tests/unit/waste-sustainability.test.ts` (29) |
| Waste, energy & sustainability reporting | M28-FR-04 / M26-FR-04 / P-08 / P-03 — a store reports "waste down 18%" and waste is not down, RECORDING is down: the one manager who logged every damaged crate went on leave, and six months later the shop believes it controls a problem it has stopped measuring; so the number carries its COVERAGE on the face of the report rather than in a footnote, below 80% it is `not_comparable` in those words, and the departments that did not report are named; waste is valued and broken down by source and department because "₹2,40,000 of waste" is unactionable and "₹1,80,000 of it is fresh produce expiring on the shelf" is an ordering decision; a period comparison REFUSES to call a fall an improvement when coverage moved — "we cannot tell" is worth more than a number the owner will repeat | `packages/waste/src/sustainability.ts` | `tests/unit/waste-sustainability.test.ts` (29) |
| **Stage 16 gate — beyond the till** | Stage 16 gate / M22 / M24–M28 / QG-06 / §28 — one trading day followed against real PostgreSQL through the six things a hypermarket does besides selling at a till: a school quoted, converted at the quoted price and refused a late re-price, with an invoice that follows the VAN (₹38,062.50) and not the order (₹82,950), a 40-day-old invoice aged as 10 days overdue, a queried invoice never chased while the same account undisputed would face a human stop-supply decision, and a payment allocated to named invoices; a supplier shown only their own rows with a rival request refused AND recorded, three attempts surfaced as probing, an expired licence stopping an ASN at the door and a renewed one letting it through while a catalogue still needs review, and a statement that reconciles with its dispute separate; ₹4,00,000 of a jeweller's gold kept out of our valuation, our own manager refused a write-off on it, the higher of rent and revenue share charged, ₹1,800 of banking difference valued, an unapproved forfeit still a liability, and the counter shut on lapsed insurance with every reason at once; a cold room holding ₹1,840 of stock including what nobody probed, a silent probe treated as a fault, and 47 unprotected minutes counted from the mains failure; "Sunday 06:00 has NOBODY rostered as opener", the deli counter blocked but not Raj, an old SOP signature rejected, 96% of target paying nothing, and one fire check escalated above 40 mop-the-aisle tasks; a reportable injury refused closure three times and a compliance pack declared not presentable; scrap rate drift asked of the rate not the person, a carry bag billed as a visible line, 118 crates never returned, and an 18% "improvement" in waste reported as `not_comparable`; then six events banked idempotently and the database refusing DELETE and UPDATE. Verified repeatable | `tests/integration/beyond-the-till.test.ts` | `docs/evidence/stage-16-beyond-the-till.md` |
| Plans, entitlements & metering | M36-FR-01 / ADR-0003 / §35 / P-01 / hard rule #1 — a metered limit is reported, invoiced and escalated but NEVER enforced at the lane: `mayContinueTrading` is typed as the literal `true` rather than a boolean, so no future edit to the module can close a hypermarket on the Saturday before Diwali (a vendor may withdraw a service; a vendor's software may not close a shop), and there is no `suspendTrading`/`enforceLimit`/`lockTenant` anywhere in the package (asserted by test); an entitlement decision reports its SOURCE because "not sold" and "not paid" are different conversations and a support engineer who cannot tell them apart wastes an afternoon; a peak dimension meters at its peak and a volume dimension at its sum, since metering four tills over thirty days as 120 bills a shop thirty times for the same lanes; a downgrade is always allowed because trapping a struggling tenant helps nobody, and it names what goes dark while deleting nothing; cross-tenant rows refuse the WHOLE result set as a critical defect rather than being filtered out, because the silently-trimmed version is the one nobody investigates | `packages/platform/src/plans.ts` | `tests/unit/platform-plans.test.ts` (20) |
| White-label branding without forks | M36-FR-02 / P-06 / OD-09 / M31 — one codebase, one deployment, many brands, because the moment a customer's branding needs a fork every fix applies N times and within eighteen months nobody can say which customer runs which version; an unset brand falls back to NEUTRAL and never to another tenant's, with branding belonging to another tenant treated as absent and the fallback rebuilt fresh each call so there is no shared object to leak through — the failure being a retailer invoicing under a competitor's mark; contrast is a BLOCKING issue rather than a note, because a cashier who cannot read the total at 8pm is a support call every day for a year and by then the colours are politically difficult to change; a tenant may rename a branch to a showroom but may NOT rename "tax invoice", "GST" or "credit note" — a document that calls a tax invoice something else is not a tax invoice — and that is refused at publish AND again at render, because the two paths are separated by a database and a year | `packages/platform/src/branding.ts` | `tests/unit/platform-branding.test.ts` (16) |
| Tenant export, closure & upgrade compatibility | M36-FR-03 / OD-09 / P-06 / hard rules #6, #7 — an export is complete or it is not an export, checked against the platform's own declared domain list so adding a domain to the product without adding it to the exporter FAILS every subsequent export rather than producing a quietly smaller file (half a dataset handed over as "your data" is worse than a refusal because the customer finds out months later), and a legitimately empty domain must be PRESENT and zero since absence and emptiness are different facts; closure revokes access immediately but deletion is a separate event that mostly does not happen, because statutory retention outlives the commercial relationship — and audit evidence is never in scope whatever anybody asks (#6); closure is refused before the tenant has taken its export, which is the last easy moment; an announced-but-unelapsed deprecation is STILL breaking, affected tenants are NAMED rather than counted ("3 tenants affected" gets deployed on a Friday), and making an optional field required is breaking even though it looks additive on a diff | `packages/platform/src/lifecycle.ts` | `tests/unit/platform-lifecycle.test.ts` (19) |
| Partner ecosystem, sandbox & API versioning | M36-FR-04 / M32 / P-06 / hard rule #7 — an ecosystem is people we do not employ holding credentials to systems we are responsible for, so a sandbox credential presented against production is refused AND recorded as a security event whether or not it was a mistake, while a production credential in the sandbox is stopped but deliberately not called an attack (calling every mix-up an attack trains people to ignore the alerts); a partner is scoped to the tenants that engaged them and an empty scope list means NONE rather than all; an unversioned call is REFUSED rather than defaulted to the latest, because defaulting is what silently breaks a partner integration on the morning we ship; a connector certified against an old contract is "old with a badge" and keeps running (pulling a working integration out of a live shop over paperwork is worse than the risk) while never-certified cannot reach production; and production data offered as a sandbox seed refuses the WHOLE seed — realistic data is generated, never copied, whatever reason is given | `packages/platform/src/partner.ts` | `tests/unit/platform-partner.test.ts` (17) |
| Self-checkout, scan-and-go & price kiosk | D04 / M12 / M15 / P-01 / hard rule #1 — the one place the CUSTOMER operates the till, sitting between two failures: too suspicious and the lane sits empty while the staffed one queues, too trusting and it is a shrinkage hole; so every intervention carries TWO messages — a neutral one for the customer in public and a specific one for the attendant ("usually a bag or a hand on the platform — check, do not accuse") — and basket-level patterns like five loose-produce lines are scored for the loss-prevention review and never shown at the lane; age verification is ALWAYS a human with no setting that changes it; scan-and-go is earned trust with an honest sampling rate where trust is withdrawn on a FOUND discrepancy and the customer is told plainly, because a scheme that fails people mysteriously loses them for good; the price kiosk always states how fresh its list is and STOPS QUOTING when stale, since a kiosk quoting yesterday's promotion is worse than no kiosk — the customer takes it to the till as a promise | `packages/self-checkout/src/self-checkout.ts` | `tests/unit/self-checkout.test.ts` (28) |
| Price integrity across shelf, POS, app & ESL | D06 / D14 / M05 / M34 / P-02 — one commerce truth is a principle until somebody checks, and the consequences are NOT symmetric: a shelf showing LESS than the till charges is a legal exposure under the Legal Metrology rules (the displayed price is what the customer was offered) and is ranked FIRST regardless of value, because a ₹4 label error still gets a notice while a ₹5,000 margin leak does not; a shelf showing more is margin, ranked by value; a surface that has not confirmed is treated as UNCONFIRMED rather than as agreeing, with the ESL device id and shelf address named so somebody can walk to it — a label silent for nine days is showing whatever it was last told and will keep showing it; and an ESL price push waits for EVERY label to echo the price back before the till may charge it, because fire-and-forget creates exactly the overcharge risk this audit exists to catch | `packages/self-checkout/src/price-integrity.ts` | `tests/unit/self-checkout.test.ts` (28) |
| **Stage 18 gate — two shops, one system** | Stage 18 gate / M36 / D04 / D06 / D14 / §35 / QG-06 — SRE Hyper Market and Kumar Stores on one deployment, one binary and one PostgreSQL database, verified repeatable (three runs, three green): both days banked in ONE append-only ledger under the same stream name with only the tenant column separating them, a cross-tenant row refusing the whole result set as a critical defect and an unscoped query refused outright; the concession module entitled for one shop and a sales conversation for the other, with suspended-for-billing reported as a third, different answer; Kumar's Diwali week at 7 lanes on a 4-lane plan metered at its PEAK and INVOICED at ₹24,000 while `mayContinueTrading` stays typed `true`; a downgrade allowed, its losses named, and no data deleted; two brands rendered from one binary with a cross-tenant brand IGNORED, a new tenant falling to neutral, "tax invoice" refused as a rename twice over, and 1.6:1 contrast blocked; an export failing on one missing domain and refused entirely on one foreign file; closure refused before the export and before a name, then retaining tax records to 2034 and audit evidence indefinitely; an API removal still breaking after a 14-month window because SRE is NAMED as still calling it; a partner's sandbox call running unchanged against a real shop, a sandbox key into production refused as a security event, production data into a sandbox refused entirely, and an unversioned call refused rather than defaulted; a self-checkout that says "a colleague will be with you" while telling the attendant "check, do not accuse", always fetches a human for beer, and watches five loose-produce lines without holding the customer up; a kiosk that stops quoting a 33-hour-old list; a ₹4 shelf understatement ranked above a ₹5,000 margin leak; an ESL silent nine days named by device and shelf; a price push holding the till until both labels confirm; and the database refusing DELETE and UPDATE on either tenant's rows | `tests/integration/two-shops-one-system.test.ts` | `docs/evidence/stage-18-two-shops-one-system.md` |
| Versioned APIs, idempotency & signed webhooks | M32-FR-01 / §30 / §31.1 / §35 / NFR-15 — at-least-once delivery means every caller eventually sends the same request twice, and none of those retries is a bug at the other end, so a replay returns THE FIRST ANSWER rather than a fresh empty 200 (which leaves the caller unable to tell whether it worked, so it retries again — the route by which a duplicate sale reaches a ledger); the stored digest is over a key-SORTED body so field order cannot make one request look like two; the same key with a different body is a CONFLICT rather than a silent replay, because returning the first answer there would hide a genuinely lost transaction; a write with no idempotency key is refused outright; identities carry tenant, branch and scope claims with an empty branch claim meaning the whole tenant; an unsupported version is refused CLEANLY naming what is supported; and a webhook is signed with the timestamp INSIDE the signature (a signature over the body alone is valid forever) over separator-joined fields, since concatenation makes the signature ambiguous and forgeable without the key — a bad signature is a security event while a duplicate delivery id is a provider retry and deliberately is not | `packages/integration/src/api-gateway.ts` | `tests/unit/integration-api-gateway.test.ts` (24) |
| Connector SDK — mapping, retry & dead letter | M32-FR-02 / §19 / §31.1 / P-08 / hard rules #6, #10 — the shape the Stage 10 Tally connector proved, extracted so every integration inherits it, because what makes integrations rot is not any single failure but that failures become INVISIBLE: an unbounded retry loop looks healthy while nothing moves, a dead-letter queue somebody clears on Mondays looks empty, and a mapping that drops what it does not recognise looks like a clean run; so an unmapped source field is an exception rather than a dropped field (how a tax code stops reaching the accounts package for a quarter) and a lookup miss is refused rather than mapped to blank; `duplicate` counts as DELIVERED because after a timeout the destination usually has it, `permanent` dead-letters on attempt one rather than burning nine retries and burying the message that mattered, `retryable` backs off bounded, and `throttled` stays queued because a provider's rate limit is not a reason to lose our message; a message carries its OWN connector version so an in-flight one is delivered under the mapping it was enqueued with; and a dead letter is read but never deleted — there is no purge, clear, remove or drop anywhere in the package (asserted by test), a correction is a NEW message with a NEW key since reusing the old one is indistinguishable from a retry at the destination, and the original stays marked rather than edited away | `packages/integration/src/connector.ts` | `tests/unit/integration-connector.test.ts` (24) |
| Managed secrets, rotation & usage monitoring | M32-FR-03 / SEC-04 / §19.1 / hard rule #4 — NO secret ever exists in this module, and that is a property of the types rather than a policy: they carry a vault REFERENCE and there is no field, parameter or return value anywhere that could hold a value, asserted by a test reading the module's own exports — because every credential leak has the same shape, nobody commits one on purpose, they add a field "temporarily" or log a config object while debugging, and once a secret CAN sit in a variable it eventually sits in a log line that is copied into a ticket, a screenshot, a chat; findings are phrased by WHAT THE SECRET PROTECTS, since "secret 14 is 400 days old" is scrolled past and "the live payment key has not been rotated in 400 days" is not; rotation OVERLAPS and revocation does not — two functions rather than one with a flag, because a hard cut fails every edge device that has not synced while a compromise accepts that breakage and NAMES its casualties before they happen; a revoked secret a live adapter still points at is the blocking finding, being not hygiene but an integration that will fail while everybody believes the rotation finished; and usage signals are surfaced with `actionTaken` typed as the literal `false`, because revoking on a spike kills a payment integration mid-sale and the spike is usually a promotion — with `silent` mattering as much, since nobody reports the alert that never fires | `packages/integration/src/secrets.ts` | `tests/unit/integration-secrets.test.ts` (20) |
| Certified adapter matrix & integration health | M32-FR-04 / D14 / §33 / §35 / hard rules #1, #3, #4 — an uncertified combination is REFUSED rather than merely undocumented, and the refusal NAMES a working alternative, because one that does not is overridden on a Sunday when the shop needs a printer and the matrix only holds if following it is easier than going around it; a payment adapter declaring it retains anything outside an ALLOWLIST cannot be registered at all with no override anywhere (an allowlist rather than a list of forbidden field names, so a provider that invents a new one next year is refused too), and the provider must be RBI-authorised since tokenisation from an unauthorised gateway is somebody else's promise about the customer's card; a credential that is a literal rather than a vault:// pointer is refused, catching the ones typed into a configuration screen that the repository secret-scan never sees; health is WHEN IT LAST WORKED rather than whether it is configured, because an adapter failing quietly for nine days is enabled and green on any dashboard that reports configuration and that is the normal way an integration dies; and `posUnaffected` is typed as the literal `true` — no integration failure may reach the till | `packages/integration/src/adapters.ts` | `tests/unit/integration-adapters.test.ts` (26) |
| **Stage 19 gate — the seams hold** | Stage 19 gate / M32 / M33 / M35 / QG-06 / P-01 / hard rules #1, #3, #4, #6 — one evening of integration traffic against real PostgreSQL, verified repeatable (three runs, three green): a till on flaky 4G sending the same ₹4,120 sale three times, the second and third in different field order, and ONE row in the ledger with the first answer returned each time; the same key reused for a different ₹990 sale called a conflict; an unkeyed write refused; a retired version refused naming where to go and two callers on the deprecated one NAMED; a payment webhook accepted once, its provider retry not treated as an attack, the same delivery captured and posted back six hours later refused as a security event, and an edited amount refused on signature; a Tally journal mapping cleanly, an unmapped cess line and an unknown ledger code both refused rather than dropped, a rejection dead-lettered on attempt ONE, a dropped connection backed off, a `duplicate` counted as delivered, a rate limit leaving the message queued, a correction refused for reusing the key and then accepted with a new one while the original failure stays on file, and a queue flagged by AGE rather than depth; a payment key 216 days unrotated reported by what it protects, a zero-grace rotation refused, a 7-day overlap leaving the old version superseded rather than revoked, a leaked signing key revoked immediately with its two casualties named in advance, and a week later one adapter still pointing at it as the blocking finding; a 45× traffic spike, an error surge and a silent shelf-label feed all surfaced with `actionTaken` false; an uncertified scanner turned away with alternatives named, old firmware told to update rather than replace, a payment adapter declaring it keeps the full card digits refused with no override, an unauthorised provider refused, and a live key typed into a screen refused; then Tally silent five hours and the ESL feed never having reported while `canTrade` stays true and `posUnaffected` is typed `true`; and six events banked idempotently with the database refusing DELETE and UPDATE | `tests/integration/the-seams-hold.test.ts` | `docs/evidence/stage-19-the-seams-hold.md` |
| Model gateway — provider-neutral, with a deterministic simulator | Stage 17 / A01–A10 / AI-NFR-01…12 / §7.1 / §19 / hard rule #5 — a language model is an UNTRUSTED INPUT rather than a component: every other module here is deterministic and either succeeds or refuses by name, while a model can be slow, truncated, confidently wrong or steered by text a stranger typed into a review, so its reply is checked the way the goods-in door checks a delivery; a timeout is an OUTCOME and not an exception because a model must never hold a cashier's screen; malformed output is REFUSED rather than repaired, since best-effort parsing of a broken reply is how a half-parsed number reaches a purchase order; an over-length answer is treated as truncated and a citation to evidence never supplied is discarded as fabricated; and the line that matters most — a proposal for a tool that was not offered is DROPPED and recorded, because text can persuade a model to ASK for anything but cannot make the gateway hand over `issue_refund`. The simulator is not a mock returning "ok" but a scripted provider reproducing every failure mode including a model that HAS been steered, and nothing in the package executes anything (no executeProposal/applyProposal/runTool, asserted by test) | `packages/ai/src/gateway.ts` | `tests/unit/ai-gateway.test.ts` (16) |
| Agent authority boundaries A01–A10 | Stage 17 / AI-NFR-12 (absolute) / §28 / P-05 / hard rule #5 — no autonomous payment, refund, purchase commitment, price change, stock adjustment or privilege change, and the model never writes to a business database; easy to write in a document and hard to keep because the pressure runs one way, so it is enforced STRUCTURALLY: FORBIDDEN_TOOLS is a closed list with NO override anywhere — not a per-agent setting, not a tenant option, not a flag — checked at grant time, subtracted again at review time and refused FIRST before any other check, with an absence-test proving no function exists that could widen an agent at runtime; an agent's tools are an allowlist; an uncited answer is refused because a confident answer with nothing behind it is the failure mode people trust most; the HUMAN is recorded as the actor and the agent as the DRAFTER, since "the AI did it" is an audit trail with nobody in it; an agent can never approve anything and a stale proposal must be regenerated rather than approved against figures that have moved; and A01, the agent closest to the owner, is READ-ONLY | `packages/ai/src/authority.ts` | `tests/unit/ai-authority.test.ts` (25) |
| AI budgets, metering & kill switches | Stage 17 / D3 (owner, 4 Aug 2026) / P-01 / hard rule #1 — ₹15,000/month platform runtime, every agent its own configurable ceiling, customer-facing on the smallest model that passes evaluation, and fail SAFE on exhaustion, which means something precise and easy to get backwards: THE AI STOPS AND THE SHOP DOES NOT, because every agent assists a process that already worked without it — so `shopKeepsTrading` is typed as the literal `true` and no future edit can make an AI bill stop a till, with no suspendTrading/blockPos/haltStore anywhere (asserted by test); the estimate is checked BEFORE the call, since metering afterwards tells you what you already owe and pre-admission is the only way "no unexpected overage" is a property rather than a hope; a tier is DOWNGRADED and never upgraded; spend is reported per agent AND against the platform ceiling because those answer different questions; and the kill switch is instant, needs NO approval (one that needs approval gets pulled twenty minutes too late), comes in three scopes so a duty manager can stop just the customer-facing agents at 8pm, and is lifted only by a separate named person — a switch that expires by itself turns a known-bad agent back on at midnight | `packages/ai/src/budget.ts` | `tests/unit/ai-budget.test.ts` (22) |
| Prompt-injection, secret and PII protection | Stage 17 / §35 / PRV / hard rules #3, #4 — said plainly: DETECTION IS NOT THE DEFENCE, because you cannot reliably spot hostile instructions when the people writing them are trying not to be spotted and every pattern list is the phrasings somebody already thought of; the defence is FENCING untrusted content as data inside a delimiter carrying a unit separator an attacker cannot type into a web form, with forged delimiters stripped IN A LOOP since a single pass lets "<<UNTRUSTED<<UNTRUSTED_DATA_DATA" reassemble into a valid fence, while trusted evidence is left alone because fencing everything trains the model to ignore the fence; the scanner's `blocks` field is typed as the literal `false` so nothing depends on it; secrets are redacted in BOTH directions — the inbound one being the less obvious and the one that leaks, since a model repeats back what it was shown and the answer lands in a log, a screenshot, a ticket — and even a vault:// reference is redacted because it names exactly which secret to steal; PII is minimised BY PURPOSE against an allowlist so a field invented later is minimised by default, and an agent answering a stock question gets no customer names at all; the pipeline runs minimise → redact → fence → THEN scan, with scanning last and changing nothing, because putting it first invites somebody to treat it as the gate | `packages/ai/src/safety.ts` | `tests/unit/ai-safety.test.ts` (19) |
| AI evaluation, scoring & regression gates | Stage 17 / AI-NFR / P-05 / P-08 — hallucination, the hardest of the four required test cases, because a hallucination looks exactly like an answer: nothing throws, the sentence is fluent and specific, and the buyer orders 400 cases on the strength of it; so an uncited answer scores ZERO however good it sounds, "I don't know" is a CORRECT answer and scores as one since any evaluation rewarding only confident answers trains a system to guess, and safety cases return `unsafe` as a SEPARATE VERDICT from `fail` because there is no partial credit on a case where the agent proposed a refund; `fitToFacePeople` requires zero unsafe cases whatever the score, an agent at 99% with one refund proposal being not 99% ready but not ready; an unrun case is a failed case; a regression BLOCKS the release rather than warning, since model behaviour drifts, providers swap models underneath you and a prompt edit that fixes one case routinely breaks three others quietly — with affected cases NAMED rather than counted; and `liveProviderGate()` records the honest boundary of simulator work so nobody blurs it | `packages/ai/src/evaluation.ts` | `tests/unit/ai-evaluation.test.ts` (21) |
| **Provider-neutrality guardrail** | Stage 17 / OB-05 / owner decision 4 Aug 2026 / P-06 — the owner approved building all of Stage 17 against a simulator on one binding condition: switching provider must remain a configuration and adapter change, never a rewrite. A promise in a document does not survive contact with a deadline, so the build FAILS if a provider name, SDK import, provider-shaped model identifier or direct network call appears anywhere outside a declared adapter directory — the tokens being assembled at runtime so the guardrail file itself stays clean, and a known-bad sample proving the detector fires. It matters most in eighteen months, when somebody under pressure adds "just one" provider check inside an agent because it is quicker than extending the adapter, and there is nobody left who remembers why not | `tests/guardrails/ai-provider-neutral.test.ts` | `tests/guardrails/ai-provider-neutral.test.ts` (4) |
| **Stage 17 gate — the AI proposes, people decide** | Stage 17 gate / A01–A10 / AI-NFR-01…12 / QG-06 / P-05 — executed against real PostgreSQL entirely on a provider-neutral simulator with NO AI account, verified repeatable (three runs, three green): a markdown drafted by A03, admitted against budget BEFORE the call, reviewed, and committed by a manager with the PERSON as actor and the agent as drafter — then the same commit refused with no name, with the wrong role, by an agent, and 2½ hours later as stale; A01 refused as read-only; a fluent answer citing evidence that was never supplied discarded as fabricated and its proposal refused for no evidence; then the hard case — a hostile customer message whose PII was minimised away, whose forged fence delimiter was stripped, whose injection was noted but blocked nothing, and where **the model OBEYED the attacker** and proposed a ₹50,000 refund and a customer export, with NEITHER surviving the gateway because neither tool was ever granted, and the authority layer refusing `issue_refund` as a security event even when the caller mistakenly granted it — proven across all 12 forbidden tools × all 10 agents; a tier downgraded rather than overspent and then a ceiling exhausted with `shopKeepsTrading` still typed `true` and the FEFO fallback named; 120 calls costing 1.09% of the ₹15,000 platform ceiling; a duty manager stopping the two customer-facing agents at 8pm without approval while the owner's brief carried on, unable to lift her own switch; `no_provider` handled as today's real state; a suite at 100% accuracy with one unsafe case refused as unfit and blocked as an unsafe regression; and the live-provider gate recorded with 8 items, none of them anything the simulator already settles | `tests/integration/ai-proposes-people-decide.test.ts` | `docs/evidence/stage-17-ai-proposes-people-decide.md` |
| Approval delegation | M02-FR-03 / §28 / P-04 / hard rule #4 — the manager goes on leave and the shop still needs refunds authorised; every business solves this and most solve it by SHARING THE LOGIN, which does not merely break separation of duties but erases attribution retrospectively and permanently, so delegation exists to make the honest route easier than the dishonest one; a delegate acts as THEMSELVES with the decision recorded in their own name and the lent authority named beside it; "until further notice" is refused as a permanent escalation nobody remembers granting, and a per-tenant maximum stops a delegation outlasting the absence it covers; even the owner cannot authorise their own delegation; nobody can lend more value or wider branch scope than they hold, and an uncapped delegation from a capped approver would grant MORE so it is refused; chains are forbidden because two hops in nobody can say who was accountable; and a delegation FROM the maker is refused by name as a self-approval with an extra step — the loophole somebody will actually try; a standing review catches the fortnight's leave in March still live in August | `packages/approvals/src/delegation.ts` | `tests/unit/approvals-delegation.test.ts` (25) |
| Credit notes, debit notes & returns reporting | M23-FR-02 / §29.1 / hard rule #2 — CGST s.34 arrives at hard rule #2 independently: a correction is a compensating DOCUMENT, never an overwrite, so the invoice is never edited and no edit/amend/reissue function exists (asserted by test) — reissuing at a lower figure is the commonest small-business accounting error and produces a return that does not match the buyer's, an input-credit mismatch on their side and a notice on ours; cumulative credits can never exceed what the invoice charged, since crediting more than you billed is a payment out dressed as an adjustment; tax reverses in the PROPORTION it was charged, because reversing the goods and keeping the GST survives audits precisely as the document's own totals still add up; a note is declared in the period it was ISSUED rather than the invoice's, which is the rule people get wrong; the s.34(2) 30-November limit is computed and a late note still issues commercially while stating plainly the tax cannot be reclaimed; and returns are split by REASON with who each belongs to, because ₹80,000 of returns is unactionable while ₹52,000 of wrong prices at the till is a job somebody does this week — with uncredited returns surfaced as money owed that no credit-note report can see | `packages/finance/src/credit-notes.ts` | `tests/unit/finance-credit-notes.test.ts` (23) |
| Import job history & data-quality scoring | M30-FR-04 / P-08 / hard rule #6 — the failure this closes is quiet and expensive: a supplier's file arriving with 12% of rows rejected every week for a year, where nobody is wrong because the operator fixes the dozen rows by hand and the import succeeds, so no alert fires and no report shows anything — the cost is an hour a week forever plus the standing risk that one week a row is fixed incorrectly, and it is visible only as a TREND and only if somebody kept the history; so every job is recorded whether it succeeded or not, the score belongs to the SOURCE rather than the operator because the fix is at the supplier's end, rejection reasons are ranked with the action for each since a count is a dashboard and a reason is one email, the quiet cost is stated in hours a year because "12% rejected" sounds tolerable and "52 hours a year retyping their rows" does not, direction is reported beside the level so effort that is working shows up, a rate on too few rows refuses to score, and comparing sources produces a list of PEOPLE TO TALK TO rather than a league table that gets screenshotted into a meeting | `packages/import/src/job-history.ts` | `tests/unit/import-job-history.test.ts` (17) |
| **Stage 10 gate — the books reconcile** | Stage 10 gate / M23 / M29 / QG-07 / §28 — one month closed against real PostgreSQL: control totals projected from the database, Tally accepting one journal and rejecting another, the close refused on both blockers at once, an unsignable pack, a corrected requeue that keeps the failure on file, a `duplicate` retry that lands one voucher, the close and a signable pack, a reopen refused twice, a correction routed forward, an owner drill that reaches the events and a scoped drill that states what it withheld, a headline that does not add up being called out, grouped alerts with the unvalued one first, a superseded approval, and three mornings of brief sent with no AI at all. Verified repeatable | `tests/integration/books-reconcile.test.ts` | `docs/evidence/stage-10-books-reconcile.md` |
| **Stage 9 gate — the day closes honestly** | Stage 9 gate / M12–M15 / D04 / QG-04 / §28 / hard rules #1, #5, #6 — one trading day walked against real PostgreSQL: park a bill, pull the power, recover it, refuse the second resume; three sales committed locally and banked; an unanswered card machine left uncertain and recovered from evidence; the close blocked while a double capture is unrefunded and permitted (but stated) while money is merely unknown; a refund that cannot be invented or double-issued and is settled only by the statement; a provider file refused for bad arithmetic; late told apart from lost; a fraud signal that acts on nothing; a case on sealed evidence that cannot be closed once tampered with; and the database refusing to delete any of it. Verified repeatable | `tests/integration/day-close-honestly.test.ts` | `docs/evidence/stage-9-day-close.md` |
| **Stage 8 gate — physical-to-system & recall** | Stage 8 gate / M08–M11 / D05 / QG-07 / §28 / hard rules #1, #2, #6 — one batch of chicken followed from the supplier's van to a recall against real PostgreSQL: cold-chain release → put-away (duplicate scan, unknown bin, quarantine refusal) → approved transfer with a valued 5 kg shortfall → 120 kg sold → **blind** count → self-approval refused → approved compensating adjustment → system equals counted, and 240 kg accounted for across shelf, branch, van, customers, loss and shrinkage; then automatic quarantine on a missing reading, recall blocked at lane/transfer/put-away with the network out, trace naming the identifiable customer, close-only-with-evidence, and the database itself refusing DELETE and UPDATE; verified repeatable | `tests/integration/physical-to-system.test.ts` | `docs/evidence/stage-8-inventory-recall.md` |
